## Migratory Birds Regulations, 2022 {#migratory-birds-regulations-2022 .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/2022-105
:::

[ENVIRONMENTAL VIOLATIONS ADMINISTRATIVE MONETARY PENALTIES
ACT](/eng/acts/E-12.5)

[MIGRATORY BIRDS CONVENTION ACT, 1994](/eng/acts/M-7.01)

[CANADA NATIONAL PARKS ACT](/eng/acts/N-14.01)

Registration 2022-05-20

Migratory Birds Regulations, 2022

P.C. 2022-523 2022-05-19
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Her Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the recommendation of
the Minister of the Environment, makes the annexed [Migratory Birds
Regulations, 2022](/eng/regulations/SOR-2022-105) pursuant to

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} subsection 12(1)[[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23733){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23733-ID0EECCAA}
  of the [Migratory Birds Convention Act,
  1994](/eng/acts/M-7.01)[[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^b^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23734){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23734-ID0EBCCAA};

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23733-ID0EECCAA){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23733}S.C.
    2009, c. 14, s. 101
    :::

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^b^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23734-ID0EBCCAA){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23734}S.C.
    1994, c. 22
    :::

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsection 16(1)[[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^c^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24199){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24199-ID0EECBAA}
  and section 17 of the [Canada National Parks
  Act](/eng/acts/N-14.01)[[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^d^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24200){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24200-ID0EBCBAA};

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^c^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24199-ID0EECBAA){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24199}S.C.
    2009, c. 14, s. 29
    :::

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^d^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24200-ID0EBCBAA){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_24200}S.C.
    2000, c. 32
    :::

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} subsection 5(1) of the [Environmental Violations
  Administrative Monetary Penalties Act](/eng/acts/E-12.5)[[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^e^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23742){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23742-ID0EBBAAA}.

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^e^](#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23742-ID0EBBAAA){#fn_81000-2-2758_hq_23742}S.C.
    2009, c. 14, s. 126
    :::
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1347561 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following definitions apply in these Regulations.

  [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Migratory Birds Convention
      Act, 1994](/eng/acts/M-7.01). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an air vehicle that does
      not have a pilot on board. ([aéronef]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[bait]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[bait]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any feed, or imitation feed,
      that may attract migratory birds. ([appât]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[chief provincial wildlife officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[chief provincial wildlife officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      person appointed as chief or director of a provincial authority
      responsible for the administration of a provincial wildlife
      act. ([agent provincial en chef de la faune]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[contact information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[contact information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means postal address,
      email address, if any, and telephone
      number. ([coordonnées]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[daily bag limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[daily bag limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
      species, or group of species, of migratory game birds or murres,
      the maximum number of birds that a person may kill or take in a
      day in a given area as set out in column 5 of Table 1 or, if
      applicable, of Table 2 of the relevant Part of Schedule 3, for
      that area and that species or, if the Minister has altered the
      daily bag limit under section 19, the limit established by the
      Minister. ([maximum de prises par jour]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[egg]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[egg]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the egg of a migratory bird and
      includes parts of the egg. ([oeuf]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[game officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[game officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
      appointed as a game officer under section 6 of the
      Act. ([garde-chasse]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[habitat conservation stamp]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[habitat conservation stamp]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a stamp
      issued for the following purposes set out in the Certificate of
      Continuance of Wildlife Habitat Canada dated July 11, 2014, as
      they relate to migratory birds:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to promote the conservation, restoration and
        enhancement of wildlife habitat in order to retain the
        diversity, distribution and abundance of wildlife;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to provide a funding mechanism for the
        conservation, restoration and enhancement of wildlife habitat in
        Canada; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to foster coordination and leadership in the
        conservation, restoration and enhancement of wildlife in
        Canada. ([timbre de conservation des habitats]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[holder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[holder]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to a permit, a
      person to whom a permit, that remains valid, was
      issued. ([titulaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[hunt]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[hunt]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means chase, pursue, worry, follow
      after or on the trail of, lie in wait for, or attempt in any
      manner to capture, kill, take, injure or harass a migratory bird,
      whether or not it is captured, killed, taken, injured or
      harassed. ([chasser]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[lure crop area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[lure crop area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area of cropland
      that, under an agreement between the Government of Canada and the
      government of a province, remains unharvested for the purpose of
      luring migratory birds away from other unharvested crops nearby
      and that is designated as such an area by a sign. ([zone de
      cultures de diversion]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[lure station area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[lure station area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area
      established under an agreement between the Government of Canada
      and the government of a province where bait is deposited for the
      purpose of luring migratory birds away from unharvested crops
      nearby, and designated as such an area by a sign. ([zone de
      diversion]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[minor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[minor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an individual who has not
      attained the age of 18 years. ([mineur]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[open season]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[open season]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
      species, or group of species, of migratory game birds or murres,
      any season during which birds of those species may be hunted in an
      area as set out in column 4 of Table 1 or, if applicable, Table 2,
      of the relevant Part of Schedule 3 or, if the Minister has altered
      the open season under section 19, the open season established by
      the Minister. ([saison de chasse]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[overabundant species]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[overabundant species]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a species of
      migratory game bird that causes damage or is likely to cause
      damage to agricultural, environmental or other similar interests
      as a result of the rate of increase of the population of that
      species or the abundance of that population, and that is is set
      out in column 2 of Table 2 of any Part of Schedule 3. ([espèce
      surabondante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a permit issued under these
      Regulations. ([permis]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[possession limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[possession limit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a
      species, or group of species, of migratory game birds or murres,
      the maximum number of birds that a person may have in their
      possession at any time in an area set out in column 3 of Table 1
      or, if applicable, of Table 2 of the relevant Part of Schedule 3
      or, if the Minister has altered the possession limit under section
      19, the possession limit established by the Minister. ([maximum
      d'oiseaux à posséder]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[preserved]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[preserved]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with respect to a
      migratory game bird, one that has

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} been eviscerated and plucked in any location
        and then been frozen, made into sausage, cooked, dried, canned
        or smoked in a location other than the hunting area;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a location other than the hunting area, had
        its edible portions removed from its carcass and then been
        frozen, made into sausage, cooked, dried, canned or smoked; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} been mounted for
        taxidermy. ([préparé]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Wildlife Habitat Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Wildlife Habitat Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the
      corporation without share capital incorporated under Part II of
      the [Canada Corporations Act](/eng/acts/C-1.8) by letters patent
      dated February 24, 1984, and continued in accordance with
      subsection 297(1) of the [Canada Not-for-profit Corporations
      Act](/eng/acts/C-7.75) on July 11, 2014. ([Habitat faunique
      Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions --- Act and Regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in the Act and in
  these Regulations.

  [[buy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[buy]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes offering to
      buy. ([acheter]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[exchange]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[exchange]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes offering to exchange,
      bartering and offering to barter. ([échanger]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[sell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes offering for sale and
      exposing for sale. ([vendre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[commercial
  transaction]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of the Act, [[commercial
  transaction]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes renting and offering to
  rent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Benefits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of these Regulations, any gift for
  which the giver receives any benefit, including a tax benefit, is
  considered a sale.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In these Regulations, unless otherwise specified,
  where a period of time is set out, the period shall not exceed 12
  months from beginning to end.

## [Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1347602 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Migratory birds

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations apply in
respect of migratory game birds, migratory insectivorous birds and
migratory non-game birds referred to in the Convention, but do not apply
to any such birds raised in captivity that can readily be distinguished
from wild migratory birds by their size, shape or plumage.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canadian waters

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** Any provision of these
Regulations that applies to a province, or any part of a province,
applies to Canadian waters adjacent to that province or that part of
that province.

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[General]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1347607 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347608 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** This Part sets out general
rules in respect of migratory birds. These rules also apply, unless
otherwise stated, to activities and permits referred to in Parts 2 to 4.

### [Prohibitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347611 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibitions

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person
  must not engage in any of the following activities unless they have a
  permit that authorizes them to do so or they are authorized by these
  Regulations to do so:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} capture, kill, take, injure or harass a migratory
    bird or attempt to do so;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} destroy, take or disturb an egg; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} damage, destroy, remove or disturb a nest, nest
    shelter, eider duck shelter or duck box.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, the following may be damaged, destroyed,
  removed or disturbed without a permit:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a nest shelter, eider duck shelter or duck box that
    does not contain a live bird or a viable egg;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a nest that was built by a species that is not
    listed in a Table to Schedule 1 if that nest does not contain a live
    bird or a viable egg; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a nest that was built by a species that is listed
    in a Table to Schedule 1 if the following conditions are met:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person who damages, destroys, removes or
      disturbs that nest provided a written notice to the Minister a
      number of months beforehand that corresponds to the number of
      months set out in column 3 of the relevant Table to that Schedule
      for the species, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the nest has not been used by migratory birds
      since the notice was received by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- baiting

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person
  must not deposit bait in an area set out in Schedule 3 during the
  period beginning 14 days before the first day of the first open season
  after July 1 of a calendar year for the area and ending on the last
  day of the last open season before July 1 of the following year for
  that area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- authorization

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), a person may deposit bait in
  a place if, at least 30 days prior to depositing the bait, they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtain, with respect to a given period, the consent
    in writing of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} every landowner, lessee, tenant and occupant
      whose land is located within 400 m of that place,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Minister, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the chief provincial wildlife officer or a
      provincial game officer who is authorized by that chief provincial
      wildlife officer to give the consent; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} post at locations that are 400 m from the place
    bait is deposited signs whose type and wording comply with the
    Minister's instructions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- banding

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) and (2), a holder of a
  scientific permit may, at any time and in any place, deposit bait for
  the purpose of banding birds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- other scientific purposes

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) and (2), a holder of a
  scientific permit or their nominee may, at any time, deposit bait
  within the confined location specified in the permit, for scientific
  purposes other than banding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sign

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person who engages in baiting in accordance with
  subsection (3) or (4) must post a sign at the place where bait is
  deposited that is of a type and wording specified in the permit and
  that indicates the number of the scientific permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Lure crop and lure station areas

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person
  must not enter a lure crop area or a lure station area unless
  authorized in writing by the chief provincial wildlife officer or the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition on hunting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not hunt a migratory game bird in a
  lure crop area or a lure station area unless the area has been
  declared open for hunting by the chief provincial wildlife officer or
  the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signs related to prohibited activities

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person
  must not destroy, damage, alter or remove a sign whose purpose is to
  prevent an activity referred to in subsection 5(1) and that is
  lawfully erected by or under the authority of the Minister or a game
  officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signs related to bait

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not destroy, damage, alter or remove a
  sign that has been placed in accordance with paragraph 6(2)(b) or
  subparagraph 61(2)(d)(i) or 62(2)(d)(i) or that designates a lure crop
  area or a lure station area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign species

**[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** A person must not introduce
into Canada for the purpose of sport, acclimatization or release from
captivity a species of migratory bird that is not indigenous to Canada
except with the consent in writing of the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession of birds and nests

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may have in their possession, for the purpose of shipping, a
  migratory bird that was killed, captured or taken in accordance with
  these Regulations or a nest that was removed in accordance with these
  Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Shipping of birds and nests

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who has in their possession a migratory bird
  or a nest under subsection (1) must ensure that it is packaged and
  that the exterior of the package is clearly marked with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the number of any permit under which the bird was
    killed, captured or taken or the nest was removed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the full name and contact information of the permit
    holder and of the current owner of the bird or nest; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an accurate statement of the contents of the
    package.

### [Temporary Possession]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347661 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession without permit

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person may, without a permit, temporarily have in their possession

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a migratory bird that is found dead, for the
    purpose of disposing of it according to applicable law, delivering
    it to a lab for analysis as soon as the circumstances permit or
    analyzing it in a lab;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an injured migratory bird, for the purpose of
    delivering it as soon as the circumstances permit to the holder of a
    scientific permit referred to in section 75 that was issued for the
    purpose of rehabilitation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an uninjured migratory bird that faces an immediate
    threat to its life, for the purpose of temporarily providing
    assistance to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application to eggs

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to eggs.

### [Permits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347670 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance by Minister

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may issue the following permits:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in
    section 30;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following damage or danger permits:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a scaring or killing permit referred to in
      section 65,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an egg or nest destruction permit referred to in
      section 70,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a relocation permit referred to in section 71;
      and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an airport permit referred to in section 72;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a scientific permit referred to in section 75;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an aviculture permit referred to in section 76;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a taxidermist permit referred to in section 77;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an eiderdown commerce permit referred to in section
    80; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a charity permit referred to in section 82.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who applies for a permit referred to in
  subsection (1) must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 32(1), pay the fee set out in
    column 2 of Schedule 2 for that permit, if any; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide the Minister with all the information that
    the Minister may require respecting the purpose for which the permit
    is to be used.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may set out in a permit conditions respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the species of migratory birds the permitted
    activity relates to and the periods of time and the areas in which
    the permitted activity can take place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the care, release, scaring, capture, killing or
    disposal of migratory birds;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the taking, relocation, handling and disposal of
    eggs;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the removal, relocation, handling and disposal of
    nests; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance of records and provision of
    information to the Minister with respect to activities conducted in
    accordance with the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift of migratory bird

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every permit is subject to the condition that the
  holder must not give away a migratory bird that is obtained under the
  permit and that is of a species listed in Schedule 1 to the [Species
  at Risk Act](/eng/acts/S-15.3) unless the Minister indicates on the
  permit that such a gift is authorized.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of feathers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every permit is subject to the condition that the
  holder must not do the following with migratory bird feathers obtained
  under the permit:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} sell or exchange them for ornamental use or
    hat-making; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} give them for ornamental use or hat-making unless
    the recipient of the gift may exercise a right recognized and
    affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/)
    to use the feather in that way.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalidity

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** No permit is valid

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of permits other than migratory game bird
  hunting permits, if it is not signed by the individual to whom it is
  issued or, if it is not issued to an individual, by a representative
  of the person to whom it is issued;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} after it is cancelled or during the period it is
  suspended, unless it is reissued in accordance with subsection 15(3);
  or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if it expires in accordance with section 16 or 33.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of the Minister --- permits

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} refuse to issue a permit to any person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cancel a permit if the Minister has reasonable
    cause to believe that the person to whom it was issued has provided
    false, misleading or incomplete information to the Minister or
    failed to comply with any condition of the permit; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} cancel, amend or suspend any permit for the
    purposes of the conservation or propagation of migratory birds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A cancellation, amendment or suspension of a permit
  under paragraph (1)(c) is effected by sending a written notice to the
  person to whom the permit was issued that includes the reasons for the
  decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation, amendment or suspension
  of permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A permit that is cancelled, amended or suspended
  under paragraph (1)(c) must be reissued

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the circumstances which led to the cancellation,
    amendment or suspension are rectified; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the person to whom the permit was issued is not
    provided with the opportunity to be heard within 30 days after the
    date of the cancellation, amendment or suspension.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** Every permit, other than
a migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in section 30, expires
on the expiry date set out in the permit or, if a permit does not
contain an expiry date, on December 31 of the year in which it was
issued.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** Every person to whom a
permit was issued and who is required by these Regulations to submit a
report to the Minister must, except as otherwise provided in these
Regulations, submit that report within 30 days of the day on which the
permit expires.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift of feathers --- permit

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to paragraph 13(3)(b), a
  person who has in their possession migratory bird feathers, other than
  eiderdown, under a permit may give those feathers to another person
  for educational, social, cultural or spiritual purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A recipient of a gift of feathers that were obtained
  under subsection (1) has the right to have the feathers in their
  possession.

### [Powers of the Minister to Vary the Application of the Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347729 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Periods or limits

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  Minister considers it necessary for the conservation of migratory game
  birds or murres, the Minister may take one or more of the following
  measures to vary the application of these Regulations in an area
  referred to in Schedule 3:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} alter open seasons, daily bag limits, or possession
    limits in the area; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prohibit hunting of a species of migratory game
    bird or murre in the area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction --- varying section 21

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, the Minister must not use the power referred
  to in subsection (1) to require that individuals exercising a right
  referred to in section 21 hunt only during an open season, to impose a
  daily bag limit or possession limit on those individuals or to
  prohibit them from hunting a species of migratory game bird or murre
  unless using the power with respect to the holders of the migratory
  game bird hunting permits referred to in section 30 is insufficient to
  achieve the conservation of those migratory game birds or murres.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power with respect to section 52

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister exercises the power referred to in
  subsection (1) in accordance with subsection (2), the Minister may
  vary the application of section 52 to have it also apply to migratory
  game birds or murres killed or taken as an exercise of a right
  referred to in section 21.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister uses the power referred to in
  subsection (1) or (3), the Minister must post a notice that describes
  the way the application of these Regulations is varied on a Government
  of Canada website.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The application of these Regulations is varied during
  the period beginning on the day on which the notice is posted and
  ending on July 31 next following that day or on any earlier date fixed
  by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister has altered the open season, the
  daily bag limit or the possession limit in accordance with paragraph
  (1)(a), a person must not, during the period referred to in subsection
  (5), as the case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hunt except during the open season established by
    the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} kill or take, in any one day, a number of migratory
    game birds or murres of any species or group of species that exceeds
    the daily bag limit established by the Minister; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have in their possession a number of migratory game
    birds or murres of any species or group of species in excess of the
    possession limit established by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- hunting in an area

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister has prohibited the hunting of a
  species of migratory game bird or murre in an area in accordance with
  paragraph (1)(b), a person must not hunt that species in that area
  during the period referred to in subsection (5).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Urgent action

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may vary or suspend the application of these Regulations in a
  given location if urgent action is needed and if the Minister
  considers it necessary for the conservation of migratory birds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister must, on a Government of Canada website,
  post a notice indicating the ways in which the application of these
  Regulations is varied or suspended and the location in which their
  application is varied or suspended.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of suspension

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application of these Regulations is varied or
  suspended during the period beginning on the day on which the notice
  is posted until the first anniversary of that day or any earlier date
  fixed by the Minister.

### [Section 35 of the [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347757 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hunting and harvesting

- **[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual exercising a right recognized and affirmed by section 35 of
  the [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/) to hunt migratory birds and
  harvest their eggs may exercise those rights without a permit and
  without being subject to a limit as to open seasons, a daily bag limit
  or a possession limit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inuvialuit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Beneficiaries of the Inuvialuit Final Agreement
  approved, given effect and declared valid by the [Western Arctic
  (Inuvialuit) Claims Settlement Act](/eng/acts/W-6.7) may, within the
  [[Inuvialuit Settlement Region]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
  section 2 of that Agreement, hunt migratory birds and harvest their
  eggs without a permit and without being subject to a limit as to open
  seasons, a daily bag limit or a possession limit.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2024-129, s. 1](#1455925-1457672){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1455925-1457672.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20220730.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to exchange

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual exercising a right recognized and affirmed by section 35 of
  the [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/) to exchange a migratory bird
  with another individual who may exercise such a right is authorized to
  do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift for taxidermy, consumption or
  training

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An individual who has in their possession a migratory
  game bird under a right recognized and affirmed by section 35 of the
  [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/) may give it to another person
  for the purpose of taxidermy, human consumption (including for
  charitable purposes) or training dogs as retrievers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift to charity permit holder

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} However, the individual must preserve a migratory
  game bird before giving it to the holder of a charity permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the limits on the possession of migratory
  game birds set out in section 46 or subsection 51(1), the recipient of
  such a gift has the right to have it in their possession.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift, sale or exchange of feathers

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual who has in their possession migratory bird feathers under a
  right recognized and affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act,
  1982](/eng/Const/) may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give, sell or exchange those feathers for a
    functional purpose; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} give those feathers to another person for
    educational, social, cultural or spiritual purposes.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Buying and possession

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} buy feathers that are sold in accordance with
    paragraph (1)(a) or exchange things for feathers that are exchanged
    in accordance with that paragraph; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have in their possession any feathers obtained in
    accordance with subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not authorize giving, selling or
  exchanging feathers for ornamental or hat-making purposes, unless the
  person who obtains the feathers may exercise a right recognized and
  affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/) to
  use the feathers for such purposes.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights held by a collectivity

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** In these Regulations, a
requirement to record the number of a permit under which an activity is
authorized is, for an individual who is engaging in that activity as an
exercise of a right referred to in section 21 or 22, a requirement to
record the name of the collectivity holding that right.

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Hunting Migratory Game Birds]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1347785 .Part}

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347786 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to the
hunting and subsequent possession of migratory game birds. Unless
otherwise indicated, it does not apply to activities that are authorized
by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in
  section 30 and described in Part 3; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} permits other than a migratory game bird hunting
  permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[[Migratory game
birds]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} including murres

**[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of this
Part, except as otherwise indicated, [[migratory game
birds]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes murres.

### [Prohibitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347793 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unauthorized hunting

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt migratory game birds unless authorized by these
  Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General hunting licence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the laws of the territory in which the
  hunt takes place, the holder of a general hunting licence referred to
  in the Wildlife Act of the Northwest Territories, S.N.W.T. 2013, c.
  30, or the Wildlife Act of Nunavut, S.Nu. 2003, c. 26, as those Acts
  are amended from time to time, may, within the relevant territory,
  without a migratory game bird hunting permit, hunt migratory game
  birds and have in their possession the birds they have hunted.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hunting out of season

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt a species of migratory game bird in an area
  referred to in Schedule 3 except during the period beginning on the
  first day of any open season for that area and that species and ending
  on the last day of that season.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Open season on certain land

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If column 4 of Table 1 or Table 2 of any Part of
  Schedule 3 indicates that an open season applies only on certain land
  in an area set out in column 1, a person must not hunt a species of
  migratory game bird set out in column 2 during the open season except
  on that land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hours hunting is prohibited

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person must not hunt a migratory game bird

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} north of 60° north latitude during the period
    commencing one hour after sunset on any day and ending one hour
    before sunrise on the next day; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} south of 60° north latitude during the period
    commencing half an hour after sunset on any day and ending half an
    hour before sunrise on the next day unless otherwise specified in
    Schedule 3.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions with respect to Sandhill
Cranes

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** If, in any calendar year,
the Minister or chief provincial wildlife officer has reasonable cause
to believe that Whooping Crane may be in any area in the province during
the open season for Sandhill Crane in the area, the Minister or chief
provincial wildlife officer may prohibit the hunting of Sandhill Crane
in that area during the remainder of the calendar year.

### [Migratory Game Bird Hunting Permit]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347810 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hunting for human consumption

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  migratory game bird hunting permit allows the holder to hunt,
  primarily for human consumption, migratory game birds other than eggs
  and to have in their possession the birds they hunted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- murres

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A migratory game bird hunting permit does not allow
  its holder to hunt a murre or to have one in their possession unless
  they are a [[resident]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} of Newfoundland and
  Labrador, as defined in section 2 of the Wild Life Act of Newfoundland
  and Labrador, R.S.N.L. 1990, c. W-8.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Habitat conservation stamp

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt migratory game birds under a migratory game bird
  hunting permit unless a habitat conservation stamp that is authorized
  by the Minister appears on the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fee for stamp

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 32(1), a person who applies for
  a migratory game bird hunting permit set out in item 1, column 1, of
  Schedule 2 must also pay the fee for the habitat conservation stamp
  set out in item 9, column 2 of Schedule 2.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The habitat conservation stamp is valid for the
  period of validity of the permit on which it appears.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hunting by minors

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Minors
  may obtain a migratory game bird hunting permit set out in item 1,
  column 1, of Schedule 2 without paying the fee set out in column 2 and
  may obtain a habitat conservation stamp without paying the fee set out
  in item 9, column 2 of that Schedule.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Must be accompanied

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A minor who holds a permit referred to in
  subsection (1) must not hunt migratory game birds unless they are
  accompanied by an individual who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is the holder of a migratory game bird hunting
    permit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has held such a permit in a previous year; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is not a minor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Number of minors accompanied

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The accompanying individual must not accompany at any
  one time more than two minors who hold a permit referred to in
  subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permit expiry

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** A migratory game bird
hunting permit expires on June 30 following the date of issue.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to have permit on person

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holder of a migratory game bird hunting permit must have the permit on
  their person while they are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hunting; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in a place other than their primary or habitual
    residence and in possession of a migratory game bird that is not
    preserved, or any murre, that was hunted under the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Must show permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The permit holder must show the permit to a game
  officer immediately on request.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Invalidity of permit after guilty finding

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any court order made
  under subsection 16(1) of the Act or a decision made by the Minister
  under section 18.22 of the Act, a migratory game bird hunting permit
  ceases to be valid when its holder is found guilty of an offence under
  the Act, other than an offence resulting from the contravention of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 5.1 of the Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sections 9 or 34, subsection 48(2), section 78,
    subsection 79(1) or (2) or section 81 of these Regulations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the following provisions, if the offence is in
    respect of migratory birds or their eggs that are used for
    avicultural purposes:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} section 5 of the Act, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 5(1)(a) or subsection 76(3), (4)
      or (7) of these Regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- application for permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any court order made under subsection
  16(1) of the Act, a person who has been found guilty of an offence
  under the Act other than an offence referred to in paragraphs (1)(a)
  to (c) must not, during a 12-month period beginning on the day that
  guilty finding is made, apply for a migratory game bird hunting permit
  unless they have first obtained the Minister's permission to do so.

### [Hunting Methods and Equipment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347852 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition -- hunting where baiting has
occurred

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt for migratory game birds within a radius of 400 m
  from any place where bait has been deposited unless the place has been
  free of bait for at least seven days or the bait was deposited in
  accordance with subsection 6(3) or (4) or an aviculture permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), the following
  areas are not considered areas where bait has been deposited:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an area of standing crops, whether or not it is
    flooded;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an area of harvested cropland that is flooded;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an area where crops have been harvested and placed
    in upright sheaves for drying in a field where they grew; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an area where grain is scattered solely as a result
    of normal agricultural or harvesting operations.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized weapons

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt migratory game birds except with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a bow that has a minimum draw weight of 18 kg and
    an arrow with a broadhead that has at least two sharp blades and is
    a minimum of 22 mm wide;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a crossbow that has a minimum draw weight of 45 kg
    and a bolt with a broadhead that has at least two sharp blades and
    is a minimum of 22 mm wide; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a shotgun not larger than number 10 gauge that
    either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is designed to be capable of holding no more than
      three cartridges, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has had its capacity reduced to three cartridges
      in the magazine and chamber combined, by means of the cutting off
      or the altering or plugging of the magazine with a one-piece
      metal, plastic or wood filler that cannot be removed unless the
      gun is disassembled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- cartridges and
  detachable magazine

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not, while hunting migratory game
  birds, have in their possession in the hunting area

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a shotgun that is holding more than three
    cartridges; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a detachable magazine capable of holding more than
    two cartridges.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- possession of shotguns

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person must not, while hunting migratory game
  birds, have in their possession in the hunting area

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} more than one shotgun, unless each additional
    shotgun is unloaded and either disassembled or kept in a closed
    case; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a shotgun other than one referred to in paragraph
    (1)(c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- single projectile

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person must not hunt a migratory game bird using a
  shotgun loaded with a cartridge containing a single projectile.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), paragraph (3)(b) and
  subsection (4), a person may, in the following circumstances, hunt a
  migratory game bird using a rifle of a calibre of not more than 0.22
  inches or a shotgun loaded with a cartridge containing a single
  projectile and have such a rifle or shotgun in their possession in the
  hunting area:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the migratory game bird is hunted in a location
    that is in the Northwest Territories by a resident of that territory
    who may hunt migratory game birds in that location without a
    migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in section 30; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the migratory game bird is hunted in a location
    that is in Quebec and north of 50° north latitude by a resident of
    that province who may hunt migratory game birds in that location
    without a migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in section
    30.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-toxic shot

- **[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt a migratory game bird

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} while having in their possession in the hunting
    area shot other than non-toxic shot; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} using shot other than non-toxic shot.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception for certain species

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to hunting American
  Woodcock, Band-tailed Pigeon, Murres or Eurasian Collared-Dove.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- Mourning Dove

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to hunting Mourning
  Dove in British Columbia.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition --- [[non-toxic
  shot]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, [[non-toxic
  shot]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any shot composed, by weight, of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} up to 100% iron, tungsten, tin or bismuth, alone or
    in any combination of those substances;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} not more than 45% copper;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} not more than 40% nickel;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} not more than 7% Nylon 6, Nylon 11 or ethylene
    methacrylic acid copolymer, alone or any combination of those
    substances; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} not more than 1% all other substances combined.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- birds and electronic bird
calls

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt a migratory game bird using

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} live birds, including non-migratory birds; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} electronic bird calls, except in an area that is
    set out in column 1 of Table 2 in the relevant Part of Schedule 3
    for which the use of an electronic bird call is set out in column 6.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- use of raptors

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite paragraph (1)(a), migratory game birds may be
  hunted using raptors in any area of a province that is designated by
  the province as an area in which a person may hunt using raptors.

### [Vehicles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347907 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- aircraft and motorized
land vehicles

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  must not hunt a migratory game bird from or using an aircraft or a
  motorized land vehicle.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- mobility-impaired person

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, a mobility-impaired person is permitted to
  hunt from an aircraft or motorized land vehicle if they are
  stationary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition --- [[mobility-impaired
  person]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (2), a
  [[mobility-impaired person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} is an individual who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the hunting occurs in a province with laws
    allowing mobility-impaired persons to hunt from a stationary
    vehicle, is authorized to do so under those laws; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the province has no such laws, holds a medical
    certificate that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is signed by a medical practitioner lawfully
      entitled to practise medicine in any province,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} attests that the individual's mobility
      impairment is due to a condition that is not temporary and that
      severely limits the use of their legs, including being paraplegic,
      being hemiplegic, being dependent on a wheelchair to move about,
      having prostheses on both legs or having a leg amputated above the
      knee, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} attests that the medical practitioner has no
      medical reason to believe that the individual is incapable of
      competently operating the weapon that they use to hunt.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- moving boats

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  must not hunt a migratory game bird from or using a moving boat that
  is equipped with a motor or a sail.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- murres

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An individual who is authorized to hunt murres may do
  so from any moving boat.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification --- retrieval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsection (1) does not apply
  to the retrieval of a migratory game bird once it is legally killed or
  wounded.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Moving

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, a boat is considered
  to be moving if it continues to move because of motion that was
  imparted by its motor or its sails.

### [Retrieving Migratory Game Birds]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347929 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Means for retrieval

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not hunt migratory game birds unless they have adequate
  means at their immediate disposal for retrieving any such bird that
  they kill or injure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prompt retrieval of killed bird

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An individual who kills a migratory game bird must
  ensure that the bird is retrieved as soon as the circumstances permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prompt retrieval of wounded bird

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An individual who wounds a migratory game bird must
  ensure that the bird is killed and retrieved as soon as the
  circumstances permit.

### [Daily Bag Limits]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347937 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  must not in any area kill or take, in any one day, a number of
  migratory game birds of any species or group of species that exceeds
  the daily bag limit in that area for that species or group of species
  as set out in column 5 of Table 1 or, if applicable, of Table 2 of any
  Part of Schedule 3.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hunting in multiple areas

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For an individual who hunts in more than one area set
  out in Table 1 or, if applicable, in Table 2 of one or more Parts of
  Schedule 3 in a single day, the limit in subsection (1) is the highest
  daily bag limit of all of the areas in which the individual hunts on
  that day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Birds found dead or wounded

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsection (1), a bird that is
  found dead and taken or found wounded, killed and taken must be
  included in the daily bag limit of the migratory game bird hunting
  permit holder who accepts to keep it, whether or not that permit
  holder hunted that bird.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- daily bag limit reached

**[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** A person must not hunt
migratory game birds of a given species once they have, on any given
day, killed or taken the number of birds of that species or group of
species that is referred to in section 43.

### [Possession]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1347947 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fully-feathered wing or head

**[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** A migratory game bird
that is eviscerated and plucked in accordance with paragraph (a) of the
definition [[preserved]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 1(1) must
continue to have a fully feathered wing or the fully feathered head
attached until the migratory game bird is being frozen, made into
sausage, cooked, dried, canned or smoked as described in that paragraph.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession limit

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a person
  must not have in their possession in any area at any time, a number of
  migratory game birds of a given species or group of species that were
  killed or taken under a migratory game bird hunting permit and that
  are not preserved that is in excess of the possession limit for that
  area and the species or group of species set out in column 3 of Table
  1 or, if applicable, of Table 2 of any Part of Schedule 3.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession limit for murres

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person must not have in their possession in any
  area at any time, a number of murres that were killed or taken under a
  migratory game bird hunting permit that is in excess of the possession
  limit for murres for that area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- multiple areas

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For an individual who has in their possession
  migratory game birds that are not preserved, or any murres, that were
  hunted in one or more areas set out in Table 1 or, if applicable, in
  Table 2 of one or more Parts of Schedule 3, other than the one in
  which the individual is located, the possession limit that applies for
  the purposes of subsection (1) or (2) is the greater of the possession
  limit for the area in which the birds were hunted and the possession
  limit for the area in which the individual is located.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Birds from other provinces

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply to an individual who
  hunted in an area that is in a province other than the one in which
  they are located unless they have on their person proof that they are
  authorized to hunt under provincial law in the area where the bird was
  hunted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift of birds

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1) or (2), a
  migratory game bird that is not preserved, or any murre, that is given
  is counted towards the possession limit of the recipient of the gift
  once they accept it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No longer counted

**[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** A migratory game bird is
no longer counted towards an individual's possession limit referred to
in subsection 46(1) or (2) when they

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} give it to another person, if the gift is accepted in
  accordance with subsection 46(5); or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a migratory bird that is not a murre,
  preserve it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary third-party possession

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person may temporarily have in their possession a migratory game bird
  on behalf of the owner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, a person must not temporarily have in their
  possession a migratory game bird for the purpose of performing
  taxidermy for profit unless they hold a taxidermist permit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Murre or unpreserved game bird

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 46, a migratory game bird that is not
  preserved, or any murre, that is possessed under subsection 48(1)
  counts towards the owner's possession limit, and not towards the
  possession limit of the person who temporarily has it in their
  possession.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction respecting murres

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The number of unpreserved murres that belong to other
  persons that a person temporarily has in their possession must not be
  greater than twice the daily bag limit referred to in section 43.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- transfer of possession

- **[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who kills or takes a migratory game bird must not allow it to
  enter the possession, including the temporary possession, of another
  person unless it is labelled or preserved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Labelling obligation of possessor

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who has in their possession a migratory game
  bird that is not preserved and that was taken under a migratory game
  bird hunting permit referred to in section 30 or as an exercise of a
  right referred to in section 21 must ensure that it is labelled unless
  that person took that bird themselves.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply if the bird was
  killed or taken by an individual who is exercising a right referred to
  in section 21 and the recipient may also exercise such a right.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Individual or group labelling

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsections (1) and (2), a bird
  is considered to be labelled if a label is attached to it or if it is
  a part of a group of birds labelled in accordance with subsection (6).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Label requirements

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The label must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} indicate

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the full name and contact information of the
      individual who took the bird,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date the bird was taken, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the number of the migratory game bird hunting
      permit under which the bird was taken; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} be signed by the individual who took the bird.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Group labelling

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Migratory game birds may be labelled as a group by
  packaging unlabelled birds in a package that is labelled or that
  contains a labelled bird, if the label satisfies the requirements set
  out in subsection (5) in respect of each bird.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Training retriever dogs

- **[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Section 46 does not apply to a
  person who is registered as a dog trainer with the Minister and who
  has in their possession migratory game birds, other than murres, for
  the purpose of training dogs as retrievers. However, the person must
  not have in their possession more than 200 migratory game birds that
  are not preserved and that were taken under a right recognized and
  affirmed by section 35 of the [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/), a
  migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in section 30, a
  provincial killing permit that is referred to in section 64 or a
  scaring or killing permit that is referred to in section 65.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- species at risk

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, the registered person must not have in their
  possession a migratory game bird that is of a species listed in
  Schedule 1 to the [Species at Risk Act](/eng/acts/S-15.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location of storage

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The registered person must store the migratory game
  birds in the location specified to the Minister at the time of
  registration, except when they are being used for dog training.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The registration expires on July 31 next following
  the date of registration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Records

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A registered person must keep records showing, in
  respect of the dead migratory game birds in their possession,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of each species and the number of birds
    belonging to each species; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the full name and contact information of the
    individual who took each migratory game bird and the number of the
    permit under which it was taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- labelling

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 50(1) does not apply in respect of a gift
  to a registered person and subsection 50(2) does not apply to such a
  person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fully feathered wing or head

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person must not have in their possession or transport a migratory game
  bird that is not preserved and that was killed or taken under a
  migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in section 30 unless at
  least one fully feathered wing or the fully feathered head is attached
  to the bird to allow its species to be identified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Identification of species

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The possessor or transporter of the migratory game
  birds referred to in subsection (1) must store them in a manner which
  allows each bird to be counted and its species to be identified.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Feathers

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  holder of a migratory game bird hunting permit may give, sell or
  exchange the feathers of a migratory game bird that they have in their
  possession under that permit for a functional purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Buying and possession

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} buy feathers that are sold in accordance with
    subsection (1) or exchange things for feathers that are exchanged in
    accordance with that subsection; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have in their possession any feathers obtained in
    accordance with subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift for taxidermy, consumption or
training

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual who hunts a migratory game bird under a migratory game bird
  hunting permit may give the bird to another person for the purpose of
  taxidermy, human consumption (including for charitable purposes) or
  training dogs as retrievers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift to charity permit holder

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} However, the individual must preserve a migratory
  game bird before giving it to the holder of a charity permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to the limits on the possession of migratory
  game birds set out in section 46 or subsection 51(1) the recipient of
  such a gift has the right to have it in their possession.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition --- abandoning

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who has in their possession a migratory game bird whose meat is
  fit for human consumption must not allow the meat

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection 9(4) of the Act, to be
    abandoned; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to become inedible for humans.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Gift

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies to a person who offers to give
  the migratory game bird to another person until that gift is accepted
  by that other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a
  migratory game bird that is used for taxidermy or for training dogs as
  retrievers.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Private conveyance

**[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** Sections 10 and 50 do not
apply to an individual who is transporting a migratory game bird on
board a private conveyance if the bird was hunted legally by an
individual who is in the private conveyance.

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Overabundance, Damage and Danger]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1348039 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1348040 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[resident]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of this
Part, [[resident]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in relation to a province,
an individual whose primary or habitual residence is in that province.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1348043 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope

**[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** This Part applies to the
management of migratory birds for the purpose of reducing the danger
that they are causing or are likely to cause to human health or public
safety or the damage they are causing or are likely to cause to
agricultural, environmental or other interests.

### [Overabundant Species]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1348046 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permit

- **[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  migratory game bird hunting permit referred to in section 30 allows
  the permit holder to kill, take and have in their possession birds of
  an overabundant species, but not the eggs of such birds.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Areas and periods

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) only permits birds to be killed in the
  areas set out in column 1 of Table 2 of any Part of Schedule 3 and
  during the open seasons set out in column 4.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Part 2 provisions that apply

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 28, 31, 32, 34, 38 and 42
  to 56 apply to an individual who kills a bird of an overabundant
  species.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Methods and equipment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 61 and 62, the prohibitions on
  hunting methods and equipment set out in sections 36, 37 and 39 to 41
  apply to an individual who kills a bird of an overabundant species,
  unless the bird is killed in an area set out in column 1 of Table 2 in
  the appropriate Part of Schedule 3 during an open season set out in
  column 4 for which that hunting method or equipment is set out in
  column 6.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hunting using electronic calls

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 39, an individual who is attempting
  to kill birds of an overabundant species using electronic bird calls
  of that species set out in column 6 of Table 2 of the appropriate Part
  of Schedule 3 during the open season set out in column 4 may, in an
  area set out in column 1, hunt migratory game birds of a species of
  migratory game birds that is not overabundant for which it is the open
  season.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Depositing bait in spring --- Quebec

- **[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  prohibition on hunting using bait set out in section 36 applies to an
  individual who kills migratory game birds of an overabundant species,
  unless the killing takes place on a parcel of land in an area of
  Quebec set out in column 1 of Table 2 of Part 5 of Schedule 3 for
  which the method and equipment set out in column 6 indicate that
  killing using bait is permitted during the open season set out in
  column 4 and, at least 30 days before the bait is deposited, the
  Minister consents in writing to the depositing of bait and the killing
  of birds of an overabundant species as set out in column 2 on that
  parcel of land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may give the consent referred to in
  subsection (1) if the Minister is provided with the following
  documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} letters of agreement, signed by the holder of a
    migratory game bird hunting permit and by the owners, lessees,
    tenants and occupants of the parcel of land on which the bait is to
    be deposited, in which those owners, lessees, tenants and occupants
    give their consent to the killing of birds by hunting on the parcel
    of land during the period set out in the letters and to the
    depositing of bait on the parcel of land for that purpose;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a map of the parcel of land that clearly indicates
    its location and dimensions and the places where bait is to be
    deposited;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} information that specifies the type of crop that is
    being or was most recently grown on the parcel of land, if any; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an undertaking in writing from the permit holder
    referred to in paragraph (a) that they will

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} ensure that, before bait is deposited on the
      parcel of land, signs whose type and wording comply with the
      Minister's instructions are posted in locations that are
      satisfactory to the Minister,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ensure that at least 1 000 kg of bait is
      deposited on the parcel of land, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} provide the Minister, within 21 days after the
      end of the open season set out in column 4 of Table 2 of Part 5 of
      Schedule 3 during which the killing took place, with a written
      report specifying the days on which the killing took place, the
      number of migratory game bird hunting permit holders involved and
      the number of birds of each species set out in column 2 that were
      killed on each day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may withdraw the consent if the permit
  holder fails to comply with an undertaking described in subparagraph
  (2)(d)(i) or (ii).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Killing near cut crops in fall --- Quebec

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  prohibition set out in section 36 applies to an individual who kills
  birds of an overabundant species of migratory game birds in Quebec
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the bird is killed in an area referred to in column
    1 of Table 2 of Part 5 of Schedule 3 for which killing using bait is
    set out in column 6;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the bait that has been deposited is crops that are
    cut and left on the ground; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} at least 30 days before the birds are killed, the
    Minister consented in writing to the killing of birds of that
    overabundant species within a radius of 400 m from the place where
    the bait has been deposited.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may give the consent referred to in
  paragraph (1)(c) if the Minister is provided with the following
  documents and information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} letters of agreement, signed by the holder of a
    migratory game bird hunting permit and by the owners, lessees,
    tenants and occupants of the land within a radius of 400 m of the
    place where crops are cut and left on the ground in which those
    owners, lessees, tenants and occupants indicate that they are aware
    that hunting migratory game birds, other than birds of an
    overabundant species, is prohibited within the radius and give their
    consent to the killing of birds by hunting of an overabundant
    species within that radius during the period set out in the letters;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a map of the place where crops are cut and left on
    the ground that clearly indicates its location and dimensions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the type of crop that is being cut and left on the
    ground; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an undertaking in writing from the permit holder
    referred to in paragraph (a) that they will

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} ensure that, before birds are killed within that
      radius, signs whose type and wording comply with the Minister's
      instructions are posted in locations that are satisfactory to the
      Minister, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} provide the Minister, within 21 days after the
      end of the open season set out in column 4 of Table 2 of Part 5 of
      Schedule 3, with a written report specifying the days on which
      birds were killed, the number of hunters involved and the number
      of birds of each species set out in column 2 of that Table that
      were killed on each day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Withdrawal of consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may withdraw the consent if the permit
  holder fails to comply with the undertaking described in subparagraph
  (2)(d)(i).

### [Birds Causing Damage or Danger]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1348089 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scaring birds

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite the prohibition on harassing
  a migratory bird set out in paragraph 5(1)(a), a person may, without a
  permit, use equipment other than an aircraft or firearms to scare
  migratory birds that are causing or are likely to cause danger to
  human health or public safety or damage to agricultural, environmental
  or other interests.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial scaring permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The chief provincial wildlife officer, with the
  concurrence of the Minister, may issue a permit to any resident of the
  province to use an aircraft or firearms, in a specified area and
  during a specified time, for the purpose of scaring migratory birds
  that are causing or likely to cause damage to crops or other property
  in the area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial killing permit

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the
  chief provincial wildlife officer and the Minister are satisfied that
  scaring migratory birds is not a sufficient deterrent to prevent the
  birds from causing serious damage to crops or other property in a
  province, the chief provincial wildlife officer may issue a permit to
  any resident of the province to kill migratory birds of a specified
  species during a specified time and in a specified area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The holder of the permit referred to in
  subsection (1) may take and have in their possession migratory birds
  killed under its authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cancellation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The chief provincial wildlife officer may cancel a
  permit issued under subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal scaring or killing permit

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may issue a scaring or killing permit only to a person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} owns, leases or manages a parcel of land;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} holds or is the authorized representative of a
    person who holds an easement, real servitude, right-of-way or
    licence of occupation in respect of a parcel of land; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has or is the authorized representative of a person
    who has rights under the laws of a province to use a parcel of land
    for public utilities or public infrastructure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rights of permit holder and nominees

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The permit describes the parcel of land and
  authorizes its holder and their nominees, within that parcel of land
  and subject to the conditions of the permit, to scare migratory birds
  with an aircraft or firearm or to kill them, if those birds are
  causing or are likely to cause danger to human health or public safety
  or damage to agricultural, environmental or other interests, and to
  take and have in their possession migratory birds killed under its
  authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nomination

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The permit holder may nominate, from among the
  residents of the province in which the parcel of land described in the
  permit is situated, as many nominees as are specified in the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A nomination by a permit holder must be in writing
  and the nominee must carry the nomination on their person at all times
  while they are performing the activities authorized by the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligations after expiry or
  cancellation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A person to whom a permit was issued must, within 15
  days after its expiry or cancellation,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} return it to the Minister; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} report to the Minister any information with respect
    to the birds killed under its authority that the Minister requires.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Validity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the cases of invalidity set out in
  section 14, a permit that was issued to prevent damage to a crop in
  the parcel of land described in the permit ceases to be valid when
  that crop is harvested.
